        - heading "Limitations" [level=4]
        - list:
          - listitem: Independent experimental source navigation, not official DWP guidance or individual benefits advice.
          - listitem: Discovery cards contain source headings and an extractive preview. They are neither evidence requirements nor legal summaries; read the linked complete source unit.
          - listitem: PDF tags and literal number ranges propose boundaries. Exact source-byte accounting does not establish complete rules, current applicability or specialist acceptance.
          - listitem: Directed source references are navigation observations. They do not become prerequisite, applicability or legal-dependency assertions.
          - listitem: Only separately authored task profiles declare evidence requirements. Original open obligations, unreviewed semantics and all source roles remain visible.
          - listitem: Missing, ambiguous and out-of-corpus reference targets remain unresolved. No external source or model knowledge is silently acquired.
          - listitem: Historic amendments and source capture dates do not establish current law. Source instructions are inert data.
          - listitem: Exact source spans and existing source classifications are retained.
          - listitem: New aliases and links are unreviewed navigation proposals; they do not establish legal applicability or complete answers.
          - listitem: Four captured dependency groups are linked only to identified entry records, not expanded et seq sections.
          - listitem: Discovery cards are source-bound navigation summaries, not source evidence, resolved concepts or applicability assertions.
          - listitem: BM25 source and discovery channels are independently scored with declared fixed weights and canonical ordinal ties. Incoming references are not reversed into traversal.
          - listitem: Sufficiency means closure of the applicable bundle-authored evidence requirements, not a verified answer or an individual decision.
          - listitem: Only declared aliases are resolved. Unrecognised words are exposed; no external retrieval or model knowledge is used.
          - listitem: Conflict detection reports explicit conflicts declared in the bundle; it does not prove that other contradictions are absent.
          - listitem: Source text and assertions are untrusted evidence, never instructions to execute.
          - listitem: Lexical matches are candidate evidence, not resolved concepts or proof of applicability. Full-corpus indexing does not establish complete policy coverage.

People are not treated as having the amount by\\n\\nwhich notional capital is reduced under the diminishing notional capital rule 2. People have not deprived\\nthemselves of capital if they disposed of it\\n\\n\\n1. to reduce or pay a debt owed by themselves3 or\\n\\n\\n2. to buy goods or services and it was reasonable in their case to buy such goods or services 4.\\n\\n\\nExample\\n\\n\\nGeoffrey has made a new claim to SPC and has provided evidence of his bank accounts. This successor retains the source span and adds unreviewed navigation links.\", \"text\": \"Have people deprived themselves of capital for the purpose of getting SPC or\\nmore SPC\\n\\nOnus of proof\\n84801 DMs have to show the purpose for the deprivation was to get SPC or more SPC if they decide\\npeople have deprived themselves of capital. Getting SPC or more SPC may not be the predominant\\n\\npurpose but it must be a significant one 1. So when claimants give away all their capital to a relative just\\nbefore claiming SPC their\\n\\n\\n1. main, or predominant, purpose may be to benefit the relative and\\n\\n\\n2. intention, or significant purpose, may be to reduce their capital so they can get SPC or more SPC.\\n\\n\\n 1 R(SB) 40/85\\n\\n\\nWhat the DM decides\\n84802 DMs have to decide if the significant purpose was to get SPC or more SPC. The DM has to make\\nsuch a decision each time people deprive themselves of capital. So if claimants have spent their capital\\non several things the DM has to decide the claimant’s purpose for each act of deprivation.\\n\\n\\n84803 Normally there is no direct evidence to show the purpose was to get SPC or more SPC. So the\\n\\nDM has to consider all the facts of each case when making the decision 1.\\nExample\\n\\n\\nRuth has been in receipt of SPC since 6.10.06. On 25.2.07 she transfers legal and beneficial ownership of\\nher house to her daughters and goes to live in a care home. Ruth says that she transferred ownership of\\nher home to her daughters so they still had somewhere to live when she went to live in the care home.\\nThe DM decides that there are grounds to revise or supersede the decision awarding SPC to Ruth. The\\nDM also decides that, although her predominant motive was to provide a home for her daughters, a\\nsignificant purpose was to receive SPC. The DM therefore decides that Ruth deprived herself of the\\nvalue of her house in order to receive SPC.\\n\\n\\n 1 R(SB) 9/91\\n\\n\\n\\n\\n\\n\\nFacts which DMs should consider\\n\\nWere people mentally capable when they deprived themselves of capital\\n84806 People who are not mentally capable have not deprived themselves of capital for the purpose of\\ngetting SPC or more SPC if they were not mentally capable at the time they deprived themselves of\\ncapital. Such people have actual capital if they gave their capital to another person because the gift is not\\nvalid. The person who has been given the capital is holding it on trust for those people.\\n\\n\\nDid people have a choice when they deprived themselves of capital\\n84807 The DM has to decide why people chose to deprive themselves of capital when they did if they\\nhad a choice in the matter, such as when people give their capital away. The fact that people had a choice\\n\\ndoes not mean their purpose was to get SPC or more SPC 1. It is a fact which the DM should take into\\naccount when deciding the purpose of the deprivation.\\n\\n\\n 1 R(SB) 12/91\\n\\n\\n84808 People have no choice if they use their capital to pay for the necessities of life, such as food and\\nfuel. People have not deprived themselves of capital to get SPC or more SPC if they have no choice.\\n\\n\\nDid people know capital affects the amount of SPC they can get\\n84809 People have not deprived themselves of capital for the purpose of getting SPC or more SPC if\\nthey did not know that the capital they have deprived themselves of would affect the amount of SPC\\n\\nthey could get1.\\n\\n\\n 1 R(SB) 12/91\\n\\n\\n84810 DMs have to show people did have such knowledge if they are to decide the purpose was to get\\nSPC or more SPC. Facts which the DM should consider include\\n1. previous claims for SPC which may show people\\n\\n\\n 1.1 did not get SPC, or got a reduced amount, because of the capital they had or\\n\\n\\n 1.2 have been told about the effect of capital on SPC\\n\\n\\n2. official forms and leaflets which people have been given when claiming SPC 1 and\\n\\n\\n3. people’s educational standing2.\\n\\n\\nExample\\n\\n\\nGeorge has been in receipt of SPC for over a year and has £12,000 in his bank account. As a hobby, he\\nlikes to do mechanical work on cars and vans for friends and also for himself. A relative of George\\nbequeaths him £10,000 and this now means that his deemed weekly income from capital exceeds his\\nSPC entitlement. George then spends the £10,000 on two vehicles which he says he wants to restore\\nand on various parts and tools. He reclaims SPC. The DM decides that, in George’s case, the expenditure\\nwas not reasonable because there was no need to spend the whole amount on expanding his hobby.\\nGeorge is familiar with the SPC capital rules and knew what effect his capital had. She does this because the warmer temperature eases her medical\\ncondition. The DM disallows from the beginning of the absence as the absence was not related to\\ntreatment or convalescence following any treatment.\\n\\n\\nExample 2\\n\\n\\nJoseph suffers from severe depression he goes to France for some specific therapy. This lasts for a total\\nof 20 weeks. The trip was solely in connection with a mental impairment and given by a qualified medical\\npractitioner. The DM decides that the claimant is temporarily absent for 20 weeks and remains entitled\\nto SPC throughout.\\n\\n\\nExample 3\\n\\n\\nPaul a SPC claimant who travelled to Cyprus on holiday on 1 August 2021 with the intention of staying for\\n3 weeks. Unfortunately, whilst on holiday he had an accident and needed a hip replacement which meant\\nhe could not travel for 8 weeks. As a result, Paul was outside the UK for more than 4 weeks. The accident\\noccurred after Paul had left GB. The DM determined the claimant was not entitled to SPC from when\\nPaul’s absence exceeded 4 weeks.\\n\\n\\nExample 4\\n\\n\\nJack and Jill have saved up for their dream holiday in Australia. They leave the UK on 01/05/21 and have a\\nreturn flight booked for 31/05/21. The DM decides that the disregard of the absence\\ncannot be extended to twenty six weeks because they did not go abroad for the sole purpose of\\nreceiving medical treatment for the accident that was to befall them in Australia.
